PART IV. NOTIFICATILONS, During the Quarter ended December 31, 1897. 24
186 CUSTOMS GAZETTE [OCT.-DEC., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 312. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. SOUTHERN ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. INTENDED OilANGE IN POSITION OF FAIRWAY BELC-BUOY. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to the movement of the South-east Spit to the south- westward, as shown by the recently completed survey of that vicinity, the Fairway Bell-buoy will be shifted I mile to the south of .its present advertised position on or about the 20th November next, its intended new position being with Gutzlaff bearing south and the Tungsha lightship N. 54vo W., magnetic. SHANGHAl, 4th October 1897. No. 313. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. PORT ARTHUR LIGHTHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given that the Port Arthur Light was re-established and its character changed on the 20th October 1897. The illuminating apparatus is now of the sixth order dioptric, occulting, showing a fixed light varied by two eclipses every 20 seconds, thus Light . . . . . . ..14 seconds. Eclipse...........2 Light.... ......... 2 Eclipse. .......2 The light shows white between N. 130 E. and N. 55 W.; red inshore of N. 13* E. and between N. 55* W. and N. 81*W.; and white between N. 810 W. and S. 4o&#39; W. The bearings are magnetic and from seaward.
1897.] NOTIFICATIONS. 187 The light is elevated 87 feet above the level of the -sea, and in elear weather the red light should be visible at a distance of 5 nautical miles, and the white light at 8 nautical miles, where not obscured by the land. The light-building, which is 7 feet high and painted white, stands close to the south- eastern side of the old lighthouse, which is painted in white and black horizontal bands. The keepers&#39; dwelling and the boundary wall are white. Approximate position:- Latitude.... . . . . 380 47&#39; 26&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . 121* 15&#39; 26&quot; E. Note.-The Port Arthur Lighthouse is in the Newchwang Customs district, but it is under the control of the Chefoo Customs. SHANGHAI, 25th October 1897. No. 314. YANGTZE R IVER-KIUKIANG. DISTRICT. FITZROY ISLAND BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Fitzroy Island Beacon Light has been shifted ato yards N. 670 E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 13th November 1897. No. 315. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. FOG SIGNALS AT LAO-T&#39;IElf-SHAN PROMONTORY LIGHTHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given that the keepers at the above station on hearing a bell, fog horn, steam whistle, or any other sound, during foggy or thick weather, which shall indicate the proximity of a vessel, will fire three guns with an interval of one minute between the first and second and the second and third, and, if the vessel&#39;s fog signal-showing that she is under way-is still heard, will repeat the firing after an interval offfteen minute8. SHANGHAI, 22nd November I897.
188 CUSTOMS GAZWE. [OCT.-DEC., 897 Nob. 316. F00HOW 0 1STR ICT. HAITAN STRAIT. RANGE BEAcoNs ESTABLISHED. NOTICE is hereby given that two range beacons have been established on the north- gastrn. slope of Pass Island, indicating a bearing of N. 20j W., to guide vessels through the narrows of Wilson Chanmel; each beacon having a diamond-shaped top-mark-the northern one with a white field and the central portion black, and the southern one with a black field and the central portion white. Also that two range beacons have been established on the suth-eastern slope of Slut Island, with the summit of the island (a boulder painted white with a vertical black stripe in the middle) and the middle of Cow&#39;s Horn Peak directly in line, bearing N. 81* W., to guide vessels in the fairway between the reefs which extend-from Mitre Rock, and the Chiu-niang Rocks, lying about 6 cables E.S.E. from Mitre Rock, the highest of which dries 8 feet at low water springs. Each of these beacons has a triangular top-mark painted white with a vertical black stripa in tly middle-the triangle on the northern one being inverted. DIRECTIONS. According to soundings obtained during the present month, a vessel bound to the north- ward should keep the beacons on Pass Island in line from the time when Quoin Hill is on with Tyne-yung-shan Peak, bearing about W. I S., till thd southern end of the middle one of the Rocky Islands is on with the fort bearing from it W. by N. k N., and then haul out so as to pass about 3 cables to the eastward of Black Rocks. The least depth found with the beacons in line was I5 feet at low water of the lowest spring tides-about half-way between the north side of &#39;Middle Island on with Town Point and the last bearing given above. The beacons on Slut Island, if brought in line when Inner Rock bears east and kept so till Mitre Rock bears west, will lead well elear of all known dangers. All bearings given are magnetic. SH,&amp;amp;XGaW, 27t&amp;amp; December 1897.

APPENDIX 1. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. OOTOBER-DEOEMBER 1897. 26
202 CUSTOM GAZETTE.[OT.-DEC. (Received s7th January 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 8TH JANUARY 1898. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instrOtions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for gublication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 1897, October-December. b. The Custom House (*4 a V) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincihl Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two&quot; Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.271 30 and Hk.Ta 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (C 8), Changehow (* g), Fotochow ( 9) and Kowloon (L fl) stations. e. The word &quot;Inwardsn in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.-The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:- i. CAPSUIMOON. JUNKS. OcTos. NovzxBz. DiCMBER. TOTAL. 1897.A. 213 (Received 19th Jrmuary 1898.) . CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 7TH JANUARY 1898. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 1897, October-December. b. The Custom House ( t U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.n 30 and Hk.Ta 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fe Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, coni menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow ( M I) and Chienshan (I&quot; th) stations, with three sub-stations, known *s the Shekkok (6 l), Kuttai (-* ), and Kwanchiap (U [) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
